					<div id="contentSub"><div id="mw-content-subtitle"><span class="mw-redirectedfrom">(Redirected from <a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_American_saints_and_beatified_people&amp;redirect=no" class="mw-redirect" title="List of American saints and beatified people">List of American saints and beatified people</a>)</span></div></div>
					
					
					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For non-Catholic American saints, see <a href="/wiki/Category:American_saints" title="Category:American saints">Category:American saints</a>. For more information on the process of sainthood in the Roman Catholic Church, see <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Canonization</a>.</div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg/220px-Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="130" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg/330px-Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg/440px-Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3203" data-file-height="1897" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" title="Basilica of the National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception">Basilica of the National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Washington,_D.C." title="Washington, D.C.">Washington, D.C.</a>, is the largest Catholic church in the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>. A special hall in the crypt level of the Basilica contains statues of American saints.</div></div></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+link+.ambox{margin-top:-1px}html body.mediawiki .mw-parser-output .ambox.mbox-small-left{margin:4px 1em 4px 0;overflow:hidden;width:238px;border-collapse:collapse;font-size:88%;line-height:1.25em}.mw-parser-output .ambox-speedy{border-left:10px solid #b32424;background-color:#fee7e6}.mw-parser-output .ambox-delete{border-left:10px solid #b32424}.mw-parser-output .ambox-content{border-left:10px solid #f28500}.mw-parser-output .ambox-style{border-left:10px solid #fc3}.mw-parser-output .ambox-move{border-left:10px solid #9932cc}.mw-parser-output .ambox-protection{border-left:10px solid #a2a9b1}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-text{border:none;padding:0.25em 0.5em;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-image{border:none;padding:2px 0 2px 0.5em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-imageright{border:none;padding:2px 0.5em 2px 0;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-empty-cell{border:none;padding:0;width:1px}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-image-div{width:52px}html.client-js body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Split plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-move" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Split-arrows.svg/50px-Split-arrows.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="17" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Split-arrows.svg/75px-Split-arrows.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Split-arrows.svg/100px-Split-arrows.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="60" data-file-height="20" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">It has been suggested that  this article should be <b><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Splitting" title="Wikipedia:Splitting">split</a></b> into multiple articles. (<a href="/wiki/Talk:List_of_American_candidates_for_sainthood" title="Talk:List of American candidates for sainthood">discuss</a>)  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">June 2021</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>This <b>list of American candidates for sainthood</b> includes not only <a href="/wiki/Saint#Roman_Catholicism" title="Saint">saints</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> but also those who are not yet recognized as saints but as <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beati</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_venerable_people_(Roman_Catholic)" class="mw-redirect" title="List of venerable people (Roman Catholic)">venerabili</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_Servants_of_God" title="List of Servants of God">servants of God</a> or <a href="/wiki/Candidates_for_sainthood" class="mw-redirect" title="Candidates for sainthood">candidates for sainthood</a> and who are significantly associated with what was either at the time or subsequently became, the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_the_United_States" title="Catholic Church in the United States">Catholicism in the United States</a> began with the first European explorations and colonization of the Americas. Indeed, <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Columbus</a>'s expedition of 1492 included Catholic priests among the crew. Catholic missionaries were some of the first Europeans to reach many parts of <a href="/wiki/French_North_America" class="mw-redirect" title="French North America">French North America</a> and <a href="/wiki/British_North_America" title="British North America">British North America</a> in the east, and <a href="/wiki/Spanish_North_America" class="mw-redirect" title="Spanish North America">Spanish North America</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Southwestern_United_States" title="Southwestern United States">Southwestern United States</a>.
</p><p>Several American Catholics have been considered for sainthood over the past 50 years. Catholics continue to contribute to American religious life. Most of these Americans were born after 1850.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />

<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Isaac_Jogues" title="Isaac Jogues">Isaac Jogues</a> (1607–1646), <a href="/wiki/Ren%C3%A9_Goupil" title="René Goupil">René Goupil</a> (1608–1642), and <a href="/wiki/Jean_de_Lalande" title="Jean de Lalande">Jean de Lalande</a> (d. 1646), Professed Priests and Religious of the Jesuits; among the North American Martyrs (France – Canada – U.S.)

<li><a href="/wiki/Frances_Xavier_Cabrini" title="Frances Xavier Cabrini">Francesca Saverio Cabrini</a> (Frances Xavier) (1850–1917), Founder of the Missionary Sisters of the Sacred Heart of Jesus (Lodi, Italy – Illinois, U.S.)
<ul><li>Declared Venerable: 21 November 1937</li>
<li>Beatified: 13 November 1938 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a></li>

<li><a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_Ann_Seton" title="Elizabeth Ann Seton">Elizabeth Ann Bayley Seton</a> (1774–1821), Widow; Founder of the Daughters of Charity of Saint Vincent de Paul in the United States, the Sisters of Charity of Cincinnati, the Sisters of Charity of Seton Hill, the Sisters of Charity of Saint Vincent de Paul of Halifax, the Sisters of Charity of Saint Vincent de Paul of New York, the Sisters of Charity of Saint Elizabeth of New Jersey (New York, U.S. – Rome, Italy – Maryland, U.S.)

<li><a href="/wiki/John_Neumann" title="John Neumann">John Nepomuk Neumann</a> (John Nepomucene) (1811–1860), professed Priest of the Redemptorists; Archbishop of Philadelphia; Founder of the Sisters of Saint Francis of Philadelphia (Prachatice, Czech Republic – Pennsylvania, U.S.)
<ul><li>Declared Venerable: 11 December 1921</li>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="American_beati">American <i>beati</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_American_candidates_for_sainthood&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: American beati">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Solanuscasey.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Solanuscasey.jpg/200px-Solanuscasey.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="296" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Solanuscasey.jpg/300px-Solanuscasey.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Solanuscasey.jpg/400px-Solanuscasey.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2361" data-file-height="3500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Solanuscasey.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Blessed</a> <a href="/wiki/Solanus_Casey" title="Solanus Casey">Solanus Casey</a> <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor_Capuchin" title="Order of Friars Minor Capuchin">O.F.M.Cap.</a>, miracle-worker and mystic.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Father_McGivney_300.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Father_McGivney_300.jpg/200px-Father_McGivney_300.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="254" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/bf/Father_McGivney_300.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="381" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Father_McGivney_300.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Blessed</a> <a href="/wiki/Michael_J._McGivney" title="Michael J. McGivney">Michael J. McGivney</a> founded the <a href="/wiki/Knights_of_Columbus" title="Knights of Columbus">Knights of Columbus</a>.</div></div></div>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Mary_Frances_Schervier" title="Mary Frances Schervier">Maria Franziska Schervier</a> (1819–1876), Founder of the Poor Sisters of Saint Francis and the Franciscan Sisters of the Poor (North Rhine-Westphalia, Germany – Ohio, USA – North Rhine-Westphalia, Germany)

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="American_venerabili">American <i>venerabili</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_American_candidates_for_sainthood&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: American venerabili">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:177px;"><a href="/wiki/File:AugustineTolton.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/2b/AugustineTolton.jpg" decoding="async" width="175" height="215" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="175" data-file-height="215" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:AugustineTolton.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Father <a href="/wiki/Augustus_Tolton" title="Augustus Tolton">Augustus Tolton</a>, the first <a href="/wiki/Afro-American" class="mw-redirect" title="Afro-American">Afro-American</a> priest of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>.</div></div></div>

<ul><li>Declared Venerable: June 11, 2019</li></ul></li>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="American_servants_of_God">American servants of God</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_American_candidates_for_sainthood&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: American servants of God">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The following list is based according to the Congregation for the Causes of Saints.

<li><a href="/wiki/Joseph_Muzquiz" title="Joseph Muzquiz">José Luis Múzquiz de Miguel</a> [Joseph Múzquiz] (1912–1983), Priest of the Personal Prelature of the Holy Cross and Opus Dei (Badajoz, Spain – Massachusetts, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Terence_Cooke" title="Terence Cooke">Terence James Cooke</a> (1921–1983), Archbishop of New York; Cardinal (New York, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Walter_Ciszek" title="Walter Ciszek">Walter Ciszek</a> (1904–1984), Professed Priest of the Jesuits (Pennsylvania, USA – New York, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>John Maronic (1922–1985), Professed Priest of the Missionary Oblates of Mary Immaculate; Founder of the Victorious Missionaries (Minnesota, USA – Illinois, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gabriel_Manek" title="Gabriel Manek">Gabriel Wilhelmus Manek</a> (1913–1989), Professed Priest of the Society of the Divine Word; Archbishop of Endeh; Founder of the Daughters of Our Lady Queen of the Holy Rosary (Nusa Tenggara Timur, Indonesia – Colorado, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Thea_Bowman" title="Thea Bowman">Thea Bowman</a> (1937–1990), Professed Religious of the Franciscan Sisters of Perpetual Adoration (Mississippi, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>María Belen Guzmán Florit (Dominga) (1897–1993), Founder of the Dominican Sisters of Fatima (San Juan, Puerto Rico – Ponce, Puerto Rico)<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>John Joseph McKniff (1907–1994), Professed Priest of the Augustinians (Pennsylvania, USA – Florida, USA – Piura, Peru)<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Rossella Petrellese (1972–1994), Layperson of the Diocese of Acerra (Naples, Italy – Minnesota, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gabriel_Gonsum_Ganaka" title="Gabriel Gonsum Ganaka">Gabriel Gonsum Ganaka</a> (1937–1999), Archbishop of Jos (Plateau, Nigeria – New York, USA)</li>
<li>Ida Peterfy (1922–2000), Founder of the Society Devoted to the Sacred Heart (Košice, Slovakia – California, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Gertrude Agnes Barber (1911–2000), Layperson of the Diocese of Erie (Pennsylvania, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Hardon" title="John Hardon">John Anthony Hardon</a> (1914–2000), Professed Priest of the Jesuits (Pennsylvania, USA – Michigan, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Leonard_LaRue" title="Leonard LaRue">Leonard LaRue</a> (Marinus) (1914–2001), Professed Religious of the Benedictines (Ottilien Congregatian) (Pennsylvania – New Jersey, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Gwen Cecilia Billings Conicker (1939–2002), Married Layperson of the Diocese of Steubenville; Cofounder of the Apostolate for Family Consecration (Illinois, USA – Ohio, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Joseph Henry Cappel (1908–2004), Priest of the Maryknoll Missionary Society (Kentucky, USA – Talca, Chile)<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Maria_Esperanza_de_Bianchini" title="Maria Esperanza de Bianchini">Maria Esperanza Medrano Parra de Bianchini</a> (1928–2004), Married Layperson of the Dioceses of Los Teques and Metuche (Monagas, Venezuela – New Jersey, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fernando_Rielo" title="Fernando Rielo">Fernando Rielo Pardal</a> (1923–2004), Layperson of the Archdiocese of Madrid; Founder of the Idente Missionaries of Christ the Redeemer (Madrid, Spain – New York, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Joseph Walijewski (1914–2006), Priest of the Diocese of La Crosse (Michigan, USA – Lima, Peru)<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>William Edward [Bill] Atkinson (1946–2006), Professed Priest of the Augustinians (Pennsylvania, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Irving Charles Houle (Francis) (1925–2009), Married Layperson of the Diocese of Marquette (Michigan, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Michelle_Christine_Duppong&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Michelle Christine Duppong (page does not exist)">Michelle Christine Duppong</a> (1984–2015), Layperson of the Diocese of Bismarck; Member of the Fellowship of Catholic University Students (Colorado – North Dakota, USA)<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
